# Spec-vs-Implementation Coverage Matrix

- **fully:** 383
- **partially:** 85
- **stub:** 20

## Gap inventory (every non-`fully` row)

### Lexer, token vocabulary, literals, typed constants, string interpolation

- **[missing]** Exponent notation restricted to `number` type — type error in integer/decimal contexts — `spec §1.3 lines 629 ('It is a type error to use exponent notation in a context that requires integer or decimal... the type checker enforces the restriction'); literal-system.md Layer 1 table ('number only')`
  - *gap:* Type checker needs an exponent-form check on numeric literals resolved to integer/decimal lanes; requires the literal's surface form (token text contains e/E) to survive into TypedLiteral or be checked at ResolveLiteral time
  - *evidence:* Probe LexProbeExponent: `field I as integer default 1e3` and `field D as decimal default 1.5e2` compile with NO error (only PRE0158 no-write-site warning). grep -i exponent over src/Precept finds no enforcement code — only SyntaxReference doc text and UCUM internals
- **[diverges]** Empty interpolation `"{}"` rejected by parser as syntax error (expected expression) — `spec §1.3 line 646 ('The parser rejects empty interpolation as a syntax error (expected expression)')`
  - *diverges:* Spec: syntax error 'expected expression'. Impl: silently accepted as a zero-hole InterpolatedStringExpression (two empty text segments). Git: ParseInterpolatedString landed with the Pratt-parser slice (9be726d7) with no empty-hole check ever present; spec text present since the docs merge (1c352fa2). AI-co-authored omission, not a decision — build the parser check
  - *gap:* Add 'StringStart immediately followed by StringEnd/StringMiddle' detection in ParseInterpolatedString (and the TypedConstant twin) emitting expected-expression; same for `'{}'`
  - *evidence:* Probe LexProbeEmptyInterp: `field H as string default "{}"` → success:true, zero errors. Parser.Expressions.cs:436–478 ParseInterpolatedString loops StringMiddle/expression until StringEnd with no requirement that any hole exists between Start and End
- **[diverges]** Lexer diagnostics reported exactly once in Compilation.Diagnostics — `spec §1 line 310 (TokenStream carries lexer diagnostics); docs/compiler-and-runtime-design.md pipeline aggregation`
  - *diverges:* Canon: each diagnostic reported once. Impl: every lex-stage diagnostic duplicated in the final Compilation.Diagnostics (and MCP output). Git: Parser's AddRange came with Slice-1 catalog parser (68075b9d); Compiler's spread predates the rename (6bb96c28 era) — two AI-co-authored slices independently claimed ownership of token diagnostics. Cleanup, not decision
  - *gap:* Pick one owner: either drop tokens.Diagnostics from Compiler.cs:24 or stop folding them into ConstructManifest at Parser.cs:52; add a no-duplicates regression test
  - *evidence:* Probe LexProbeMinimal: single unterminated string → PRE0002 appears TWICE. Root cause: Parser.cs:52 returns manifest.Diagnostics = tokens.Diagnostics.AddRange(state.Diagnostics), then Compiler.cs:22–30 aggregates [..tokens.Diagnostics, ..manifest.Diagnostics, ...] — lexer diagnostics included twice
- **[partially]** `~` + other identifier in expression position → specified diagnostic: ExpectedToken with '{0}=startsWith or endsWith', '{1}=actual', Tilde+identifier span — `spec §1.1 line 521 (exact diagnostic contract)`

- **[missing]** `~` before a non-string type (e.g. `field X as ~integer`) caught by parser or type checker — `spec §1.5 line 519 ('Invalid uses (e.g., ~ before a non-string type ...) are caught by the parser or type checker'); §1.1 line 517 (Tilde valid on ~string scalar and collection inner string only)`

- **[partially]** List-literal defaults accepted for all canonical collection kinds (set, queue, stack, log, bag, list) including empty [] — `collection-types.md line 779 ('default [...] | set, queue, stack, log, bag, list'); collection-types.md:115/869 (`set of string default []`); literal-system.md Layer 4 examples (`set of string default ["pending"]`, `queue of number default [85, 90, 78]`)`

### Expression language — operators (arithmetic/comparison/logical/CI/ternary), built-in functions, quantifiers, presence, member access (spec §2.1, §3.5–3.9; Operators/Operations/Functions/ExpressionForms catalogs)

- **[diverges]** Exponent-notation numeric literals (1.5e2): number-only typing, type error in integer/decimal context, correct value — `spec §1.3:621-629, §3.8:1632 ('exponent literal in integer/decimal context → TypeMismatch')`
  - *diverges:* Spec: exponent form is number-only, error elsewhere, value = mantissa×10^exp. Impl: typed integer/decimal by dot-presence, value silently 0, no lane enforcement — and the wrong 0 flows into proof-engine interval/default folds (PRE0164 witness E=0), so proofs reason on wrong constants (false-reject observed; false-accept plausible). Git: code from typed-literal slices (b7868d60, 82a92056, refactor f148ca21); no commit records dropping exponent support; the discarded TryParse is the tell — AI-co-authored oversight → cleanup, not decision
  - *gap:* Add exponent-aware parse (NumberStyles.Float) + number-lane-only enforcement diagnostic + expected-type propagation for number contexts; add proof-engine regression test that an exponent literal's value is honored
  - *evidence:* Probes: `rule N >= 0.0e0` → PRE0018 'Expected a number value, got decimal' (exponent literal typed decimal); `field I as integer default 1e2` / `field E as decimal default 1.5e2` accepted with NO TypeMismatch; PRE0164 then witnesses 'fails when E=0' — the literal VALUE silently parses to 0. Root cause: TypeChecker.Expressions.cs:191-215 ResolveNumericLiteral uses decimal.TryParse(NumberStyles.AllowDecimalPoint) / long.TryParse and DISCARDS the failed parse (`_ =`), yielding 0; no exponent branch exists

### Graph analyzer (reachability, dead-end detection, dominators/required, terminal analysis, guard treatment, event coverage, outcome classification, field-write-site analysis, proof forwarding)

- **[partially]** Irreversible-modifier enforcement (PRE0110 IrreversibleStateHasBackEdge) — `spec §0.5 item 5 (line 188): 'there is no PATH from this state back to any ancestor state'; modifier table line 423: 'no path back to any ancestor state'; graph-analyzer.md §6.4 + resolved-note (BFS-ancestor)`
  - *diverges:* Spec (owner-authored 2026-04-29, commit a52282a8, pre-implementation) demands no-path-back — i.e. genuine reverse-reachability ('Reverse-reachability. Required for irreversible'). Impl (2026-05-07, 67d42b2a) shipped a direct-back-edge-only check; the same-day team-autonomous doc resolution (Wave 4) canonized 'BFS-tree ancestor' for the ancestor notion but never addressed direct-edge-vs-any-path. Git read: AI-era implementation under-delivery against the pre-existing owner spec target, not a deliberate weakening decision — cleanup direction is to strengthen the impl, not relax the spec.
  - *gap:* Implement true reverse-reachability: forward-reach set from each irreversible state intersected with its ancestor set (or with all states ordered-before it) must be empty; flag the first edge on any returning path. Reverse adjacency and BFS machinery already exist (ComputeDeadEnds). Also decide BFS-tree-ancestor vs any-earlier-reachable-state as the ancestor notion and align spec+doc wording.
  - *evidence:* BuildBackEdgeViolations inspects only DIRECT edges whose FromState is the irreversible state, against BFS-tree ancestors (GraphAnalyzer.cs:626–668). Probe 1 (direct): A→B(irreversible)→A → PRE0110 Error. Probe 2 (multi-hop): A→B(irreversible)→C→A, A→Go→B re-enterable → compiles CLEAN, no PRE0110 — the irreversibility claim is structurally violated but unflagged.

## Canon inconsistencies reported (doc-drift to fix, do not silently resolve)

- Spec section numbering: §1.7 heading is missing — spec jumps from '### 1.6 Dual-Use Token Disambiguation' (line 708) to '### 1.8 Lexer Diagnostics' (line 776). The interpolation mode-stack content (lines 749–774) sits unnumbered inside §1.6, yet lexer.md §15 cross-references 'precept-language-spec.md §1.7, §1.8' for interpolation syntax.
- Spec §1.2 keyword list (lines 580–600) is labeled 'The complete v2 reserved keyword set' but (a) contains `writable`, which §1.1 line 331 declares retired and which the Tokens catalog does not contain (probe confirms it is an ordinary identifier), and (b) contains all the v3 keywords (modify, readonly, editable, bag, list, log, lookup, by, at, for, ascending, descending, append, insert, put, countof, peekby) that the immediately-following 'v3 additions (not in v2)' paragraph says are NOT in v2. The list is the merged v2+v3 set minus the retirement; the label and the additions paragraphs contradict it.
- Newline structural role: spec §1.4 (line 685) says NewLine tokens 'are trivia to the parser rather than structural boundaries' and Tokens.cs:31 agrees ('NewLine tokens are stripped before the parser'), but lexer.md §11 'Newline as Token' says 'Newlines are structurally significant to the grammar — they terminate statements. The parser needs to see them.' Direct contradiction between two canon docs; the implementation/spec side (trivia) appears current, lexer.md stale.
- lexer.md §7 'Two-character operators' list (line 333) omits `<-` (BackArrow): it lists only ->, !~, ~=, ==, !=, >=, <= while spec §1.1/§1.5, the Tokens catalog, and the lexer's derived table all include `<-` (probed working via computed fields).
- lexer.md §6 TokenMeta field table documents fields `TextMateScope`, `SemanticTokenType`, and `IsValidAsMemberName` — the actual TokenMeta (Token.cs:35–69) has a single `VisualCategory: SemanticTokenTypeKind` from which both tooling scopes derive, and IsValidAsMemberName is a computed property over Types-catalog accessors, not a stored flag; min/max are marked `IsFunctionCallLeader`, a field lexer.md doesn't mention.
- Spec §1.8 recovery table row for unterminated interpolation ('resume in the enclosing literal mode at the next line') omits that the enclosing literal then immediately emits its own unterminated-literal diagnostic on the same newline (two diagnostics per incident) — behavior lexer.md §9 documents explicitly and the implementation produces; the spec's 'scan forward for a } at depth 0' clause is implemented only for the depth-limit case, which is the only case where it can apply.
- literal-system.md Layer 4 and spec §1.3 present `set of string default ["pending"]` / `queue of number default [85, 90, 78]` as current language, and collection-types.md:779 lists six collection kinds for `default [...]` — the implementation accepts it only for `list`. Whichever way the gap is closed, three canon docs currently overstate the implementation in an 'Implemented'-adjacent register (literal-system.md status says 'Implemented for typed-constant validation' which is accurate for that clause, but the Layer 4 examples carry no not-yet-built marker).
- choice field-vs-field comparison: primitive-types.md line 347 ('Comparing two choice fields — even with the same member set — is a compile-time error') directly contradicts spec §3.6 line 1398 (`<` valid on two ordered choices with same element type + order-preserving subsequence). Impl follows neither (accepts everything ordered).
- choice backing: primitive-types.md §choice describes string-only backing ('Backing: String value constrained to a declared finite set', variants table shows only `choice of T(...)` with string examples) while spec §2.3 ChoiceElementType + §3.8 + the Types.cs ChoiceElement trait define five element types (string/integer/decimal/number/boolean). primitive-types.md §choice needs the multi-element-type story.
- sqrt(integer): spec §3.2 / primitive-types.md lane rule 2 + context matrix say integer→number widening is implicit 'in any context' including function arguments, but spec §3.7 sqrt row says 'integer inputs are type errors'. One of the two must yield.
- integer backing/overflow story is three-way inconsistent inside primitive-types.md: §Approximation Stance says 'Arbitrary-precision whole numbers; no overflow', §integer says 'Overflow is a type error' and '+ … Checked overflow', and §Edge-value behavior says integer range is long.MaxValue/MinValue. Catalog hover says 'arbitrary precision'.
- Diagnostic stage labels: spec §3.10 design note says EmptyChoice (46), ChoiceMissingElementType (90), ChoiceElementTypeMismatch (88) are DiagnosticStage.Parse detected in ParseTypeRef; impl emits EmptyChoice/DuplicateChoiceValue from the type checker (TypeChecker.cs:769/779) and the other two are dead — stage claims drift either way once the dead codes are revived.
- spec §3.8 routes 'comparison on unordered choice' to TypeMismatch (type stage); the shipped mechanism is a proof-stage Ordered modifier obligation (PRE0112). Behavior matches, taxonomy doesn't.
- spec §3.8 lists ChoiceElementTypeMismatch for 'choice of integer arg to choice of string field', but the live implementation reports that case as ChoiceArgOutsideFieldSet (PRE0087) — if PRE0087 is the intended surface, the spec row is stale; if PRE0088 is intended, the impl is misrouted.
- temporal-type-system.md § Summary table (lines 157-158) lists 'Temporal quantity or ISO duration/period' as canonical parse shapes for duration/period ('PT72H', 'P30D'), contradicting the locked v3 decision (lines 681, 777: 'Duration and period have no constructor literal form'; ISO notation is 'specialist serialization syntax designed for machines'). Impl is split down the middle: period accepts 'P30D', duration rejects 'PT72H'.
- temporal-type-system.md § Collection inner types: the table (1732-1743) marks set of period/timezone/zoneddatetime ✗ ('no natural ordering') while the paragraph directly below (1745) declares them valid with only .min/.max as type errors ('the prior restriction was more restrictive than necessary'). Impl follows the paragraph.
- temporal-type-system.md § Summary table line 157 gives duration canonical serialization as 'PT72H' (ISO), but § duration Serialization (742) specifies NodaTime DurationPattern.JsonRoundtrip '-H:mm:ss' ('72:00:00') — two different canonical serializations for the same type.
- Decision numbering collision: the instant section embeds a 'Locked Decision #21 — now() is UTC-only' (line 596) while the Locked Design Decisions section's #21 is 'inZone as the sole timezone mediation operation' (line 1581). Two distinct decisions share the number.
- spec:1408/1411 names UnqualifiedPeriodArithmetic as the diagnostic for unconstrained-period arithmetic, while the catalog allowlist (DiagnosticCoverageAllowLists.cs) records it as never wired and the live behavior is proof-stage PRE0113 — the spec, the diagnostic catalog, and the implementation each tell a different story about which code owns this rejection.
- Catalog-vs-doc dayOfWeek: temporal-type-system.md (462, 1025) specifies ISO Monday=1/Sunday=7; the catalog accessor description (Types.cs:14) says '0=Sunday'. Since the catalog is itself canon (machine-readable spec), this is canon-internal, not just impl drift.
- temporal-type-system.md line 126 includes the single-component unit-hole form 'H[magnitude] T( ) H[unit]' in the closed temporal grammar, but no temporal-unit-typed expression exists anywhere in the language surface to inhabit that hole (impl wires unit holes to unitofmeasure only) — the grammar claims a form the rest of the canon gives no way to express.
- exchangerate qualifier form: business-domain-types.md § exchangerate documents 'in <cur>/<cur>' compound declarations, interpolation 'in {A}/{B}', and slash-literal semantics where .to is matched against the money operand; spec §2.3 grammar (1116) declares TypeQualifier := (in|of|to) and the catalog (Types.cs:46-50, UsageExample :684) implements 'in <from> to <to>' with .from matched against the money operand. The two canon surfaces disagree; impl follows the spec grammar.
- D16 function-inheritance table vs spec §3 function overloads: D16 grants floor/ceil/truncate to money/quantity and abs/round/clamp/min/max to price (with § price prose directing authors to round(Rate,N)); the spec function table (1566-1605) and the catalog provide money/quantity overloads only and route floor/ceil/truncate as decimal|number→integer. One of the two must move.
- Curated dimension-category enumeration: § UCUM dimension categories table lists 7 categories with force/speed on the post-v1 watchlist, while § Approximation Stance (line 180) lists 10 (adding force, speed, count); DimensionCatalog implements the 10. Also 'count' is declarable in of '...' (with its own PRE0138 bounds rule) but absent from the documented of-category set.
- Bare-number bounds: § Field Constraints table shows 'min 1'/'max 100' bare-integer bounds on quantity, while D16 requires min/max bound constants to be 'the same domain type as the field, with matching unit/currency'. Impl splits the difference (quantity accepts bare, money rejects).
- Doc example units are not UCUM codes: § of category table and teachable messages use 'mi', 'lbs', 'fl_oz' style spellings; D5 says validation is against the UCUM grammar, and the impl rejects 'mi' (valid codes are [mi_i], [lb_av]). The examples cannot compile as written.
- unitofmeasure atomic-only rule (§ unitofmeasure: structural characters rejected) vs the catalog's own UnitOfMeasureValidation examples ('m/s^2', 'mg/dL') — the machine-readable spec contradicts the prose spec.
- exchangerate .from accessor description: § exchangerate accessor table calls .from 'Source currency (USD in USD/EUR)' while the conversion-example prose says the RESULT matches .from — within the same doc section the from/to roles are described inconsistently (independent of the impl divergence).
- money/price → quantity (MoneyDividePrice=204) and price/quantity (PriceDivideQuantity=203) exist in the catalog (machine-readable spec) and work, but appear in neither business-domain-types.md operator tables nor the spec §3 summary table.
- 'maxplaces currency.minorUnit' contextual form (PRE0152/PRE0035) shipped (F-LANG-BIZ-10) but no canonical doc documents it; canon still describes maxplaces as integer-literal-only.
- Diagnostic-code mapping: business-domain-types.md § Diagnostic Code Reference maps field-vs-field cross-currency/cross-dimension to CrossCurrencyArithmetic/CrossDimensionArithmetic and content errors to InvalidCurrencyCode/InvalidDimensionString; the impl emits PRE0114 (proof path) and PRE0052/0053 (typed-constant path) for those shapes, with PRE0070/71/76/77 live only on narrower paths.
- Direction-modifier applicability: spec §1.2 token table (line 334) says `ascending` applies to 'queue of T by P and log of T by P (v3)' while `descending` (line 335) and collection-types.md grammar (lines 73-81, 466-473) restrict direction to queue of T by P only — does log-by admit ascending?
- `.at(N)` proof requirement: spec §3.7 accessor table (lines 1522/1526/1530) lists `count > 0` for at(N); collection-types.md (lines 336/716/940) lists `N >= 0 and N < F.count` (which entails non-emptiness). The impl requires both; canon docs disagree on the requirement
- Cross-kind action diagnostic naming: collection-types.md per-type Type-errors tables label wrong-collection-kind actions `CollectionOperationOnScalar` (e.g. 'enqueue SetField → CollectionOperationOnScalar') AND label scalar-set-on-collection `ScalarOperationOnCollection`, but the impl's code names map the opposite way (PRE0048 ScalarOperationOnCollection fires for wrong-kind-on-collection; PRE0047 CollectionOperationOnScalar for collection-action-on-scalar) — the doc's own naming is semantically inverted for the wrong-kind rows
- Quantifier implementation status: collection-types.md §Quantifier Predicates (line 801) says 'Parser support ships first… Not yet implemented' while spec §2.2.3/§3.5 present quantifiers as shipped grammar with full binding rules, and the implementation is fully live — the collection doc's status line is stale
- collection-types.md log declaration example (line 306) `field AuditTrail as log of string notempty` reads, in context (the log section discussing non-emptiness discharge), like collection-level non-emptiness, while the same doc's Constraint Catalog (line 775) locks notempty as element-routed — the example invites the misreading the PRE0064 message also makes
- Lookup quantifier binding: collection-types.md line 1252 sketches `.key`/`.value` for lookup as 'the principle generalizes' but never locks it; the doc neither permits nor forbids quantifying a lookup, and the impl ships a scalar-V binding — under-specified surface
- spec §3.8 Access mode validation table (precept-language-spec.md:1726-1727, 1729-1730) still references the retired `writable` modifier and a `WritableOnEventArg` diagnostic, while spec :331 declares writable retired in favor of editable, :1057-1065 describes the unified editable keyword, and the impl ships EditableOnEventArg (PRE0041). Spec-internal drift; impl follows the §2.2/§2.4 side.
- spec §1.2 Reserved Keywords (:583) and the v2-additions list (:602) still include `writable`, contradicting spec :331's retirement note and Tokens.cs (no writable token).
- spec §3.8 type-compat table (:1628) says comparison on an unordered choice emits `TypeMismatch` at type-check; the proof-engine contract (§0.6, BUG-012 fix record) and the impl treat it as a proof-stage ModifierRequirement obligation (PRE0112 UnprovedModifierRequirement). The two canon statements name different stages/codes for the same violation.
- spec §2.4:1138 claims a constraint modifier 'participates in compile-time proof identically to the equivalent rule', but canon elsewhere (locked relational-rules design, §0.6 implementation-status drift already on record) acknowledges rules themselves do not yet participate at write sites — the §2.4 equivalence claim has no defined behavior to be equivalent TO for the flag modifiers (nonnegative/positive/nonzero) until that design lands.
- collection-types.md:791 asserts `set C = [literal]` is type-rejected because 'a list literal is legal only in default' — true in event-handler positions, but the impl's PRE0044 gate misses global rule bodies (probe: list literal in a rule compiles), so the canon assertion overstates current enforcement; and collection-types.md:779/:115/:869-871 specify list-literal defaults on six collection kinds while spec §3.8's list-literal table (:1758-1764) is silent on which collection kinds accept them — the impl rejects all but `list`, satisfying neither cleanly.
- spec §1.1 token table line 412 glosses `all` as 'Universal quantifier / modify all / omit all', but the quantifier grammar (§2.2:1021-1022, collection-types.md:840) is each|any|no and `each` (line 414) is the universal quantifier — probe confirms `all t in Tags (...)` is a parse error; the line-412 gloss is stale/misleading
- spec §3.8:1628 says unordered-choice comparison → TypeMismatch (type-check stage), while §3.6:1398 frames choice ordering as an operand requirement the impl realizes as a proof-engine Modifier obligation (PRE0112) — the two sections imply different stages/codes for the same rejection and neither names the shipped PRE0112
- Quantifier binding type for `lookup of K to V` (and `log of T by P`) is unspecified: spec §3.5:1364 says 'the collection field's inner type' and the collection-types binding-rules table gives a projection only for `queue of T by P`; collection-types.md:10 claims quantifier predicates span all nine kinds — K-vs-V-vs-projection is a genuine canon gap (impl currently binds V for lookup)
- spec §3.7 function-catalog markdown table is structurally broken: the CI-functions blockquote at line 1593 interrupts the table, detaching the toLower/toUpper/left/right/mid/now rows into a separate fragment
- spec §2.1:869 and §3.7:1590 require '~startsWith first arg must be ~string; type-checker errors otherwise (see §3.8)', but the §3.8 ~string-enforcement table defines no row or diagnostic code for that case — the cross-reference dangles (and the impl enforces nothing)
- spec §3.8:1683's recommended idiom for 'non-empty if present' (`ensure Arg is not set or Arg.length > 0`) is not dischargeable under the implemented when-guard-only narrowing model; it only appears to work for event args because optional-arg presence obligations are never generated — canon recommends a pattern its own proof contract can't currently honor
- Binding-vs-binding shadowing in nested quantifiers (`each x in A (each x in B (...))`) is unaddressed by §3.5/collection-types (only field-shadowing is specified); impl silently allows it
- spec §0.6 'Implementation status' table (dated 2026-05-24, lines ~235-247) lists obligations 7 (contradictory rule), 8 (vacuous rule), 9 (dead guard PRE0082), 10 (tautological guard) as 'Specification-only' — but probes show PRE0155, PRE0154, PRE0082, and PRE0153 all FIRE today (Tests 9/12). The table is stale in the impl-ahead-of-spec direction; the narrative §0.6 text below the table already describes parts of the satisfiability scan as live. The table also still cites 'Compiler Readiness Plan (Phase 5)' tracking IDs (F-LANG-SPEC-NN), which the new readiness plan will supersede.
- spec §3.5 expression-scope table (line 1344) says 'Ensure condition / guard: All field names' with no mention of event args, while §3.5 'Event arg access' (line 1376) and the §3A.1 examples (line 1884) grant event ensures access to the anchor event's args. The table under-specifies; one row should split state-ensure vs event-ensure scope.
- spec §0.6 relational paragraph says an unconditional rule participates 'exactly as a same-row when X >= Y guard does' while the same-slice implementation makes the row guard and the rule mutually exclusive fact sources (Strategies.cs:1127-1129) — promoted spec text and shipped code from the same slice (e7faae3a / 7ebbb68c) disagree.
- Samples (e.g. samples/academic-course-registration.precept) use because-clause interpolation as a first-class idiom and spec §2.5/§3.6 define interpolation type-checking, but no spec section explicitly states the because clause's interpolation semantics (what scope holes resolve in, when they render); the impl discards holes entirely. The spec defines `because StringExpr` by grammar but never pins the StringExpr semantics for because specifically.
- reject reason: spec §2.2 :979 grammar says `reject StringExpr` and §3.8 :1770 specs a TypeMismatch backstop, but Outcomes.cs (the machine-readable spec) declares RequiredStringLiteral — the two halves of canon disagree; impl follows the catalog (literal-only), making the §3.8 TypeMismatch row unreachable
- stateless handler guards: spec §2.2 :1004 says 'Handlers do not support when guards', but §3A.5 :2085 argues guards on `on <Event>` are 'semantically coherent' and Constructs.cs EventRow carries an optional guard slot; PRE0014 EventHandlerDoesNotSupportGuard exists but has no emission site (dead)
- spec §3.8 access-mode validation table (:1719-1731) still uses the retired `writable` keyword and the name `WritableOnEventArg`; §2.2 :1062 records that `writable` was retired for `editable`, and the impl diagnostic is EditableOnEventArg (PRE0041) — table is stale
- transition reject rows: spec §2.2 grammar (:962-976) permits `(-> ActionStatement)* -> reject ...`, but Constructs.cs TransitionRowReject (:197-205) has no ActionChain slot — canon disagrees with itself about whether pre-reject actions exist; impl accepts them (semantically dead mutations)
- `dequeue F by H` semantics: spec :1709 says H is a key SELECTOR ('dequeues the entry whose key matches H'); Actions.cs DequeueBy hover + ActionSlotRole.OrderingCapture say `by` is an output CAPTURE of the ordering value — mutually exclusive semantics; impl implements neither (operand dropped from the typed model)
- PRE0146's message text ('add on Create -> ... or an initial-state transition row') advertises the legacy initial-state-transition-row lane that the spec's fire-once section (:2095-2099) categorically forbids via PRE0145
- spec §2.2 stateless event hook grammar (:1000-1008) omits both the guard and the reject form that the EventRow/EventRowReject catalog constructs and the implementation support
- graph-analyzer.md §6.1 expands `from any` into terminal states (making PRE0109 unavoidable whenever a precept has terminal states and a wildcard row), while the spec is silent on wildcard-vs-terminal interaction — internally consistent but a likely-unintended trap worth an explicit canon statement
- spec §3A.5 :2068-2071 enumerates four construction-enforcement diagnostics but omits ConstructionGuardReadsUninitializedField (PRE0148), ZeroConstructionRows (PRE0146), and MultipleInitialEvents (PRE0147), all of which are implemented and load-bearing
- name-binder.md §7.5 + §11 (Q7, 'Forward-Reference Detection in NameBinder': computed fields may only reference earlier-declared fields, forward ref → UndeclaredField) contradicts spec §3.5 scope table (computed expression sees ALL fields except dependency cycles). Implementation follows the spec (probe P5 clean; dependency-ordered resolution since e68008d0). Stage doc is stale.
- name-binder.md §7.3 ('Event args shadow fields in event context' for bare identifiers; scoping order bindings > args > fields) contradicts spec §3.5 ('Dotted notation is the ONLY way to reach an event arg... a bare identifier always names a field') and type-checker.md Decision 20. The binder code still implements the old order internally (NameBinder.cs:748–763), the type checker the new one — so the two stages' resolution disagrees on collisions.
- Computed-field cycle detection ownership: type-checker.md §6 sub-pass 2c ('Computed field cycle detection — DFS') and §13 plan Slice 6 place it in the TypeChecker; it is implemented in the NameBinder (NameBinder.cs:286–317), acknowledged only by a comment at TypeChecker.Validation.Structural.cs:89.
- spec §3.3 uniform rule ('If no context is available... diagnostic and ErrorType. No implicit fallback') vs type-checker.md Numeric Literal Context Resolution / Decision 17 ('Bare numeric literals resolve to integer by default' + one-retry). Implementation follows D17 (probe P32: `rule 1 > 0` clean).
- spec §3.8 uses `writable` and `WritableOnEventArg` (access-mode validation table, lines 1726–1727) while §3.8's own modifier table row says 'editable (access modifier)' and the implemented keyword/diagnostic are `editable`/EditableOnEventArg (PRE0041); the parser rejects `writable` outright. Spec is internally inconsistent on the modifier's name.
- type-checker.md PRE0137 section cites 'TypeChecker.Expressions.cs:1207-1241' and claims operator-family-uniform enforcement; the lines are stale (check now at :1348–1382) and the check is dead for declared quantity operands (pre-empted by the :1293–1296 early return added in a03fcf4e). Doc asserts behavior the code no longer has.
- spec §3.8 choice table lists ChoiceMissingElementType and ChoiceElementTypeMismatch as firing checks while §3.10 design notes classify them Parse-stage; in practice neither fires on field declarations (probe P14) — the spec's two descriptions and the behavior are three different stories.
- spec §3.8 'Empty list as default | Valid — empty collection' + collection-types.md:779 (`default [...]` applicable to set/queue/stack/log/bag/list, with inline examples `field Tags as set of string default []`) vs spec §3.8's mechanism being implemented only for `list of` — canon promises a surface on five collection kinds that the impl rejects with a type error (probes P21/P32).
- spec §0.5 item 4 vs graph-analyzer.md §10.3: spec names 'O(V+E) via Lengauer-Tarjan' as the dominator algorithm; the stage doc's locked decision is the opposite ('Lengauer-Tarjan is unnecessary' — simple iterative at ≤50 states). Impl follows the stage doc. Spec wording needs reconciling to the locked decision (or vice versa).
- spec §0.5 item 3 vs graph-analyzer.md §6.2: two different dead-end definitions — spec says 'all outgoing rows reject or produce no-transition'; stage doc (and impl) say 'reachable non-terminal with no structural path to any terminal' (strictly broader). One definition should win in both docs.
- spec §0.5 item 5 vs graph-analyzer.md §6.4: spec demands 'no PATH from this state back to any ancestor' (reverse-reachability); stage doc and impl check only DIRECT back-edges from the irreversible state to BFS-tree ancestors. This is both a canon inconsistency and an implementation gap against the spec target (probe-confirmed multi-hop escape).
- spec §0.5 item 6 wording: 'events whose every row is reject/no-transition' — taken literally would false-positive on legitimate no-transition-only cross-cutting events (e.g. trafficlight sample's VehiclesArrive); impl flags reject-only. Spec wording should say 'reject'.
- graph-analyzer.md §4/OQ2 vs spec construction-event model (§ construction rows, post Slice 8b): stage doc claims EventHandlers are never read and handlers are stateless-only; spec/impl have construction rows as TypedEventHandler entries in stateful precepts, consumed by the graph analyzer in three places.
- graph-analyzer.md §4 proof-fact consumption table vs proof-engine impl: doc claims all five fact subtypes are consumed (ledger recording, guard-completeness reasoning); only ReachabilityFact + DeadEndStateFact are consumed; doc also omits the implemented dead-end→dead-end obligation suppression it does NOT describe.
- graph-analyzer.md §12 resolved-note on GraphEvent.IsInitial (structural edge derivation) vs spec/impl construction-event semantics (declared `initial` event modifier) — note predates the construction-event redesign.
- graph-analyzer.md Appendix + §6.2 vs shipped diagnostics: PRE0119/PRE0125/PRE0126/PRE0158 and the Message A/B dead-end split are implemented but undocumented in the stage doc (status 'Implemented' overclaims doc completeness in the other direction).
- precept-language-spec.md §0.6: the Implementation-status table (lines 239–245) says obligations 7–10 are 'Specification-only', but the prose immediately below (:251–258) and §5 describe implemented satisfiability behavior — and probes confirm PRE0155/0154/0082/0153 all emit. Only row 12 is accurate. Also a numbering collision: the paragraph 'Sequential proof flow (item 7) — implemented' (:251) refers to Proof-philosophy #7 while the table's '7' is obligation #7 (contradictory rules) — two different 'item 7's in one section.
- precept-language-spec.md:247 claims 'proof attribution … implemented and exercised by the proof-engine test suite' while item 13's required shape (every proven range carries contributing constraints/rules) has no carrier in ProofLedger — the claim overstates what exists.
- docs/compiler/proof-engine.md states three different strategy counts: §2/§4/§14 say 'six strategies', §13 says both 'all six strategies' and 'five-strategy coverage validation', while §5/§7 document 11 strategies + 2 requirement-dispatched paths (matching source).
- docs/compiler/proof-engine.md §4 Right-Sizing: 'Estimated LOC 400–600' vs an implementation spanning ProofEngine.cs + 9 partial files — the right-sizing table is a design fossil presented as current.
- docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md carries three inconsistent arm/code counts in one section: '144 arms — one per DiagnosticCode member', '(all 115 arms present in source)', and '164 active diagnostic codes'. Source truth: 164 members.
- docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md enum listing omits ~17 members that exist in source (codes 112–118, 120–126, 130–132) while asserting 'The enum IS the complete set of diagnostic rules.'
- docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md FaultCode listing shows 13 members; source has 15 (LengthBoundViolation=14, CountBoundViolation=15) — and the doc's own §DiagnosticStage section describes CountBoundViolation's fault backing, contradicting its own enum listing.
- docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md: Boundaries section says 'Roslyn rules PRECEPT0001–PRECEPT0031'; the enforcement-chain section says '24 Roslyn rules (PRECEPT0001–PRECEPT0024)'. Source: IDs run 0001–0031 (29 analyzer classes; 0029/0030 are stale-allow-list companions, 0031 is the modifier-axis rule confusingly hosted in a file named Precept0029ModifierAxisDisjoint.cs).
- docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md §Emission shapes claims Pattern 3 (dispatch residue) is enumerated in DiagnosticCoverageScanner.cs; the scanner implements Patterns 1–2 only — Pattern-3 codes are instead hidden in Gate1AllowList with factually wrong comments ('no emission site wired' for DivisionByZero/SqrtOfNegative, both probe-confirmed emitting).
- docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md documents FaultCode.UnexpectedNull as [StaticallyPreventable(NullInNonNullableContext)] while NullInNonNullableContext is marked retired/never-emitted in Gate1AllowList — the chain's intent ('every fault has an emitted preventing diagnostic') is internally broken for this member.
- docs/compiler/proof-engine.md §13 'Validation Required' and 'Blocking Dependencies' sections still describe initial-state satisfiability as unimplemented/blocked ('Implementation requires the type checker's expression resolution engine to be operational') while §7 documents it as shipped — stale within one doc.
- diagnostic-system.md quotes PRE0114's message as "'A' (Currency: 'USD') and 'B' (Currency: 'EUR') measure different things and don't combine…" — implemented message is 'Cannot combine … their Currency qualifiers don't match. Align both declarations, or insert an explicit conversion…'.
- spec §1.7 does not exist — numbering jumps §1.6 (line 708) to §1.8 (line 776); the lexer mode-stack content (lines 749-774) sits headerless inside §1.6 (heading lost in an edit, content intact)
- spec §2.7:1206 lists EventHandlerDoesNotSupportGuard as a live parser error while Constructs.cs:177-185 gives EventRow a guard slot, the parser has zero emission sites, and guards on event handlers compile clean; diagnostic-system.md:219 also lists code 14 with NO retirement annotation — both canonical docs contradict the deliberate retirement recorded in docs/Working/Archive/constructor-semantics.md:943 ('parser no longer emits it'). (The fill-task brief's claim that diagnostic-system.md already marks code 14 retired-pending-removal is itself wrong — verified absent.)
- spec §2.7 lists UnexpectedKeyword, InvalidCallTarget, ChoiceMissingElementType, and ChoiceElementTypeMismatch as live diagnostics; none has any emission site in src/Precept (all lost in the 004eede1 catalog-first parser rewrite) — and the two choice codes guard validation that is entirely absent (invalid choice declarations compile clean)
- parser.md §Output Shape carries a locked 'Decision (2026-05-06): SlotValue subtype inventory is 17' while ConstructSlot.cs/SlotValue.cs have 20 — stale by the doc's own 'code enum is authoritative' rule; the slot-payload table is also out of date (ParsedModifier/ParsedAction/ArgumentSyntax shapes)
- catalog-system.md Level 1 topology counts drifted: Constructs 15 vs 14, Modifiers 29 vs 28, Diagnostics 162 vs 164; its Exhaustiveness-Strategies CS8509 table cites methods that no longer exist (BuildNode, ParseSetStatement/ParseEmitStatement/ParseFailStatement, InvokeSlotParser) and says '11 expression forms' vs 15

# Full per-area rows (all 619)

## Lexer, token vocabulary, literals, typed constants, string interpolation

*Docs read: docs/language/precept-language-spec.md §1 (Lexer: §1.1–§1.8, lines 308–813); docs/compiler/lexer.md (full, 758 lines); docs/compiler/literal-system.md (full, 651 lines); docs/language/collection-types.md (default [...] rows: lines 115, 779, 869); src/Precept/Language/Tokens.cs (catalog); src/Precept/Language/TokenKind.cs (catalog); src/Precept/Language/Token.cs (TokenMeta); src/Precept/Pipeline/Lexer.cs (implementation)*

**Area summary:** Lexer/token/literal area is the strongest-conforming part of the compiler: all 130+ token kinds, the catalog-derived recognition tables, string/typed-constant segmentation, escapes, interpolation mode stack, all 8 lexer diagnostics with recovery, dual-use tokens (set/min/max/in/of), keyword retirements, and the 64KB guard are fully implemented and probe-verified against spec §1. Five real gaps: (1) every lexer diagnostic is double-reported in Compilation.Diagnostics (Parser.cs:52 + Compiler.cs:24 both add tokens.Diagnostics — cleanup bug, probe shows PRE0002 twice); (2) empty interpolation \"{}\" compiles clean though spec §1.3 mandates a parser syntax error; (3) exponent notation is accepted in integer/decimal contexts though spec §1.3 mandates a type error (no enforcement code exists); (4) `~` on non-string types (`field X as ~integer`) is silently accepted though spec §1.5 says it must be caught; (5) list-literal defaults work only for `list` fields — set/queue/log/bag (and empty `[]`) reject with PRE0018, contradicting collection-types.md:779's six-kind table while the proof engine already carries TypedListLiteral default count obligations. One minor diagnostic-contract mismatch: `~foo(...)` rejects via PRE0030 unknown-function instead of the spec-specified ExpectedToken('startsWith or endsWith'). Git reads on all divergences point to AI-co-authored omissions/overlaps (cleanup/build), not deliberate decisions. Seven canon-internal inconsistencies reported, headline ones: missing §1.7 heading, the 'v2' keyword list containing v3 keywords plus retired `writable`, and spec-vs-lexer.md contradiction on whether NewLine is parser-structural.

## Primitive types (string/~string, boolean, integer, decimal, number) + choice types: declarations, conversions/widening, operators, accessors, literals, constraints, primitive built-in functions

*Docs read: docs/language/primitive-types.md (full, 732 lines); docs/language/precept-language-spec.md §1.1 (type/operator/literal keywords), §1.2, §1.3 (numeric/string/typed-constant literals), §2.3 (TypeRef/ChoiceType grammar), §2.4 (field modifiers), §3.2 (widening), §3.3 (context-sensitive resolution), §3.6 (expression typing), §3.7 (function catalog), §3.8 (semantic checks: ~string enforcement, modifier validation, choice validation), §3.9, §3.10; src/Precept/Language/Types.cs (catalog: String/Boolean/Integer/Decimal/Number/Choice metas, WidensTo, Traits, ChoiceLiteralTokens); src/Precept/Language/Functions.cs (full function catalog incl. TildeStartsWith/TildeEndsWith); src/Precept/Language/Operations.cs (CI operation entries, CIDiagnosticCode); src/Precept/Language/DiagnosticCode.cs; docs/Working/bugs.md (BUG-017..032)*

**Area summary:** Primitive-type core is in strong shape: all six types, the three-lane numeric model (widening, lane separation, bridges, assignment/comparison/function/default contexts), string/boolean operator surfaces, modifier structural validation, ~string ==/!=/startsWith/endsWith/contains enforcement, and choice's five element types with ordered-rank comparison all verify by probe. The gaps cluster in five places. (1) Literal resolution: exponent literals silently resolve to value 0 in every context (TryParse without exponent styles, failure discarded — a silent wrong-value soundness bug), the exponent number-lane-only restriction is wholly unenforced, and the spec'd no-context-literal diagnostic doesn't exist (silent integer/decimal fallback). (2) Choice validation regressions: ChoiceMissingElementType and ChoiceElementTypeMismatch were built in the choice-of-T spike (d308bcd4) and silently dropped by the catalog-first parser rewrite (004eede1) — both are now dead codes; choice DEFAULT membership is unchecked (confirmed false-clean); and field-vs-field choice comparison accepts disjoint sets and even mismatched element types (canon itself is split on the rule). (3) CI-qualifier propagation: if/then/else unification drops ~string's CI flag (spec says preserved), and ~startsWith/~endsWith never enforce the first-arg-must-be-~string rule. (4) Dead diagnostic FunctionArgConstraintViolation: round(x,-1) and mid(s,0,-1) compile clean. (5) Diagnostic quality: the PRE0018 template misrenders in several shapes ('Expected a sqrt value here'), and pow's exponent rejection misroutes to SqrtOfNegative via threshold-shape-keyed code selection. Seven canon-internal inconsistencies reported, the most decision-needing being the choice field-vs-field comparison contradiction and the sqrt(integer) §3.2-vs-§3.7 conflict.

## Temporal types (date, time, instant, duration, period, timezone, datetime, zoneddatetime) + temporal arithmetic + component accessors + now()

*Docs read: docs/language/temporal-type-system.md (full, 1938 lines); docs/language/precept-language-spec.md §1 keywords (465-476, 592-602, 614), §3.3 typed constants (1304-1307), §3.4 operators (1399-1457), accessors (1537-1539), functions (1599), modifier applicability (1668-1672), §3.10 diagnostic catalog (1832); docs/Working/bugs.md (temporal entries BUG-003/008/009/010/012); src/Precept/Language/Types.cs (temporal catalog entries 427-561); src/Precept/Language/Operations.cs (temporal ops 266-414); src/Precept/Language/OperationKind.cs; src/Precept/Language/Time/* (TemporalParser, TemporalQuantityParser, TemporalUnits, TemporalValidator); src/Precept/Language/Diagnostics.cs (temporal codes 514-562)*

**Area summary:** Temporal surface is substantially implemented and catalog-faithful: all 8 types, the full per-type operator matrix (Operations.cs:266-414 matches the canon matrix cell-for-cell including absences-as-rejections), accessors including period .basis/.dimension, .inZone mediation with strict no-skip-level hierarchy, now() arity/UTC rules, of/in period qualification with proof-engine prove-or-reject (PRE0113/0141/0068), duration division safety, and degenerate-period-comparison warning. Three high-impact gaps dominate: (1) BROKEN FLAGSHIP FORM — `date ± '<quantity>'` and `time ± '<quantity>'` literals (incl. interpolated '{n} days') hard-fail with wrong-type content-validation errors (PRE0056/0057/0122) because ResolveBinaryOp's unique-RHS-candidate heuristic (TypeChecker.Expressions.cs:984-995, from AI-co-authored phase-3 commit e07a084e) falls back to the LHS type when ≥2 catalog candidates exist (date has Period+Time; time has 3) and the early error-return forecloses retry — untested, unused in samples, pure cleanup; (2) teachable-diagnostics layer largely unbuilt: 4 of 8 spec'd temporal codes (PRE0059-0062) are dead/allowlisted, TEMP message/suggestion text is swallowed into generic PRE0053 for timezone/quantity families, PRE0056's template echoes the invalid input as its own fix, and all cross-type rejections render generic PRE0018 instead of canon's ~25 field-type-aware messages; (3) timezone validation accepts legacy aliases ('EST') that canon mandates as errors, with no deprecation warnings. Two genuine divergences need owner rulings tied to canon-internal contradictions: ISO 'P30D' acceptance (period-only, deliberate code vs locked no-ISO-literal decision) and the temporal unit-hole grammar form. dayOfWeek 0=Sunday catalog description contradicts canon ISO numbering (must fix before evaluator lands). Runtime evaluator/serialization stubbed, out of scope.

Root cause: TypeChecker.Expressions.cs:984-995 — the proactive context pass picks a unique RHS type only when exactly ONE catalog candidate exists for (op, lhs); Date+ has two (Period via DatePlusPeriod, Time via DatePlusTime) and Time+ has three, so it falls back to left.ResultType (Date/Time), the typed constant hard-fails content validation, and the error-propagation early-return at :1016 forecloses the context-retry at :1037. instant/zoneddatetime/datetime LHS work because their (op,lhs) candidate is unique.

## Business-domain types (money, currency, quantity, unitofmeasure, dimension, price, exchangerate) + in/of/to qualifiers + UCUM conversion + maxplaces/precision

*Docs read: docs/language/business-domain-types.md (full, 2091 lines); docs/language/precept-language-spec.md (§0.6 item 5, §1.1 keywords/types, §1.3 typed constants, §1.6 in/of disambiguation, §2.3 TypeRef/TypeQualifier grammar, §2.4 modifier↔type table, §3 operator/function/accessor tables ~1296-1675, §3.10 refs); docs/Working/bugs.md (Active + BUG-030/031/032 sections); src/Precept/Language/Types.cs (catalog entries for all 7 types); src/Precept/Language/OperationKind.cs + Operations.cs (business operator rows); src/Precept/Language/Functions.cs (business overloads); src/Precept/Language/DiagnosticCode.cs + Diagnostics.cs (codes 23, 67-77, 113/114/117/121/124, 133-141, 150, 152, 157, 160-162); src/Precept/Language/Ucum/* (UcumAtomCatalog tiers, DimensionCatalog, AffineOffset)*

**Area summary:** Business-domain coverage matrix: 67-row audit of the 7 types + in/of/to qualifiers + UCUM + maxplaces, grounded in 14 precept_compile probes plus catalog/source reads. Core surface is solidly implemented: all 7 types with catalog-declared qualifier shapes and accessors, typed-constant validation, in/of exclusivity, of-dimension enforcement, currency/unit narrowing (with correct or-branch refusal), money/quantity/price algebra with decimal-scalar enforcement, D15 period cancellation incl. PRE0074, composite-basis machinery (PRE0160/161/162), bounds qualification (PRE0133/134/138), cross-unit bound normalization via UCUM factors, maxplaces incl. the undocumented currency.minorUnit form, and exchangerate's implicit positive. Major gaps/divergences: (1) SOUNDNESS false-accepts — BUG-030 cross-unit cancellation re-confirmed (USD/kg × g proves), cross-counting-unit +/==/< proves clean (PRE0137 fires only on function/division paths; spec itself calls it a known bug), and a new finding: interpolated typed-constant holes ('{Amt} {Cur}' into money in 'USD') generate ZERO assignment-qualifier obligations — fail-open. (2) Over-rejects — money*exchangerate commutative order never discharges (catalog declares BidirectionalLookup), price×duration cancellation never discharges even for time-denominated prices, dimension-narrowed and cross-unit commensurable assignments rejected despite D8/canon examples, .basis facts don't bridge to temporal-dimension. (3) Missing — time-denominator compound quantities ('kg/hour') rejected at declaration (no NodaTime-vocab bridge in UCUM qualifier parsing), money/money→exchangerate and quantity/quantity→compound derivations are dead catalog rows (overload selection ignores QualifierMatch.Different), inverse compound division, compound interpolated price qualifier, Tier-1 static interpolation resolution, floor/ceil/truncate on money/quantity and all price function overloads (canon-internally contested), clamp with typed-constant args. (4) Exchangerate declaration/literal/direction semantics diverge from business-domain-types.md (impl 'in X to Y' + from/to literal order is the deliberate, spec-grammar-aligned design since the first catalog commit — the doc is stale). 10 canon-internal inconsistencies catalogued for the readiness plan to resolve before building.

## Collection types (set/queue/stack/bag/list/log/logby/queueby/lookup), inner-type value modifiers, ascending/descending, accessors, lookup-access (F for K), cardinality (mincount/maxcount)

*Docs read: docs/language/collection-types.md (full, 1555 lines); docs/language/precept-language-spec.md §§0.4.1/0.6/1.2/2.2.3/2.3/2.4/3.5/3.6/3.7/3.8 (collection-relevant sections via targeted reads); docs/Working/bugs.md (full Active section + relevant Fixed entries BUG-016/018/019/027); src/Precept/Language/Types.cs (collection TypeMeta + accessor catalog); src/Precept/Language/Actions.cs (action catalog); src/Precept/Language/ActionKind.cs; src/Precept/Language/Operations.cs (LookupAccess, Contains); src/Precept/Language/DiagnosticCode.cs (collection code group)*

**Area summary:** Collection-types area: 38 features audited against collection-types.md + spec §§2.3/3.6/3.7/3.8 + catalog, with 20 precept_compile probes. Core surface is strong: all 9 kinds, accessor/action catalogs, emptiness obligations (PRE0063/64), count-bound proof (PRE0136, both directions), element value modifiers (write-prove + read-carry), forward propagation, and quantifiers (each/any/no incl. queue-by .value/.by) are fully live. Five SOUNDNESS-class false-cleans confirmed by probe: (1) `F for K` never generates its KeyPresenceSafety obligation — PRE0099 is dead code (unguarded lookup access compiles clean); (2) dequeue/pop `into` targets and `put` K/V and `enqueue-by`/`append-by` axes are completely untyped (wrong-type captures compile clean; into-targets also invisible to write-site analysis → false PRE0158); (3) wrong-currency element writes into qualified collections are unchecked (the established qualifier-source-mutation hole); (4) mincount 1 discharges accessor safety on a collection that is provably empty forever (mincount-at-birth false-Proved); (5) unqualified `set of money`.min accepted (kind-level orderability) while `period in 'days'` is wrongly rejected. Two headline DIVERGES: list-literal defaults work only on `list of T` (canon: 6 kinds; git shows AI-co-authored Slice-9 under-implementation = cleanup) and direction modifiers parse on any collection kind unvalidated. Also MISSING: collection-in-interpolation rejection (PRE0051 defined, zero emission sites), ordering-key orderability validation, PRE0151 emission. The doc's own canonical `.at` guard example fails to compile (impl demands an extra count>0 conjunct). Six canon-internal inconsistencies reported, including inverted cross-kind diagnostic naming and the stale 'not yet implemented' quantifier status.

## Field/arg modifiers (optional, editable, ordered, notempty, nonnegative, positive, nonzero, min/max, minlength/maxlength, mincount/maxcount, maxplaces, default) + modifier-type compatibility matrix + desugar-to-rules semantics — spec §2.4/§3.8, Modifiers.cs catalog

*Docs read: docs/language/precept-language-spec.md §2.4 (1131-1158), §3.8 Modifier validation (1661-1696), §3.8 type-compat/access-mode/computed/list-literal tables (1616-1782), §1.2 (576-612); src/Precept/Language/Modifiers.cs (full, 399 lines); docs/language/collection-types.md §Constraint Catalog (779-800), §List literals (866-884), per-kind constraint notes (115, 165-426, 994-1356); docs/Working/bugs.md (BUG-017/018/019/020/021/022/027/028/031, full status read); src/Precept/Pipeline/TypeChecker.Validation.Modifiers.cs (full); src/Precept/Pipeline/ProofEngine.Intervals.cs (190-300); src/Precept/Language/Actions.cs (270-475); src/Precept/Pipeline/Parser.cs (533-613)*

**Area summary:** Modifier surface audit (15 value modifiers + editable, compat matrix, desugar semantics): the declaration-site validation layer is essentially complete and catalog-driven — applicability (PRE0033, all 16 probe cases), bound pairs (PRE0034), value validity (PRE0035), duplicates (PRE0036), redundancy incl. type-implied (PRE0037), optional+notempty conflicts on fields and args (PRE0120), computed/event-arg editable (PRE0038/0041), and the qualified-bound family (PRE0133/0134/0138/0152) all fire correctly. The proof-participation layer is where the spec target is unmet: min/max and minlength/maxlength enforce on writes (with a misleading PRE0078 overflow message for plain bound violations), mincount/maxcount enforce on mutations but mincount is never established at construction (probe-confirmed false-Proved: born-empty queue discharges 'must be non-empty' from its own mincount), and nonnegative/positive/nonzero are read-side facts only — provably violating writes compile clean (deliberate half-step from the BUG-017 fix; write side is BUG-021 + the locked relational design). Non-literal modifier bounds (`min OtherField`, `min 2+3`) are silently inert (BUG-020 + one unfiled shape). Three outright misses: choice-default membership unchecked, default-expression interiors collect no fault obligations (1/0 compiles), and list-literal defaults on set/queue/stack/log/bag are rejected (canon specifies six kinds; only list works — never-built, with the proof-side default-count machinery already waiting). PRE0044 misses rule bodies; ordered-choice enforcement diverges to proof-stage PRE0112 and is fail-open in guards. DesugarsToRule is declared in the catalog but consumed by no pipeline code — the generated-rationale half of §2.4 is a stub. Five spec-internal inconsistencies recorded, led by the retired-`writable` residue in §3.8/§1.2.

- **[diverges]** default [...] list-literal defaults on non-list collections (set/queue/stack/log/bag) — `collection-types.md:779 ('default [...] | set, queue, stack, log, bag, list'), :115 `set of string default []`, :869-871 examples; :791 default-count obligation`

BUG-018's fix even built the default-count proof machinery assuming these defaults exist.

Lookup rejection is per-canon correct. Root: TypedListLiteral types as TypeKind.List and IsAssignable (TypeChecker.Expressions.cs:1644-1649) has no list-literal→collection rule. Meanwhile the proof engine ALREADY generates the canon-specified default-count obligation (probe: CountContainment 'default of G has 1 element(s)' Proved on the very declaration the type checker rejects).

But in a global `rule` body (`rule ["p","q"] contains "p"`) → NO diagnostic, list literal resolves and compiles. Emission is gated on ctx.CurrentEventName != null (TypeChecker.Expressions.Callables.cs:620-628) — rule bodies have no current event.

TryGetComparableModifierValue (TypeChecker.Validation.Modifiers.cs:462-480) handles only literal/unary-minus/typed-constant; everything else silently drops.

Read-side only: FlagLowerBound folds nonnegative/positive ⇒ ≥0 into operand intervals (ProofEngine.Intervals.cs:208-216, comment: 'it never creates a rejection'); nonzero contributes nothing (NotEquals hole, :225). Defaults ARE checked (PRE0079 on `nonnegative default -10`).

## Expression language — operators (arithmetic/comparison/logical/CI/ternary), built-in functions, quantifiers, presence, member access (spec §2.1, §3.5–3.9; Operators/Operations/Functions/ExpressionForms catalogs)

*Docs read: docs/language/precept-language-spec.md §1.3 (literals), §2.1 (precedence/null-denotation/left-denotation), §3.5 (scope + quantifier binding), §3.6 (expression typing rules), §3.7 (function catalog), §3.8 (semantic checks), §3.9 (error recovery); docs/language/collection-types.md (§Quantifier Predicates, §F for K / KeyPresenceSafety rows 654/719/746/1321-1370, §String Interpolation, §List Literal Defaults, §log of T by P append uniqueness); src/Precept/Language/Operators.cs (full); src/Precept/Language/OperationKind.cs (full enum); src/Precept/Language/Operations.cs (cross-currency/price rows); src/Precept/Language/Functions.cs (full); src/Precept/Language/ExpressionForms.cs (full); src/Precept/Language/DiagnosticCode.cs (expression-relevant codes)*

**Area summary:** Audited the expression language against spec §2.1/§3.5–3.9 + Operators/Operations/Functions/ExpressionForms catalogs with 16 precept_compile probes. Core surface is in good shape: the operator set (arithmetic incl. %, comparisons + non-associativity, and/or/not, ~=/!~, ternary, contains, for, is set), the full 23-function catalog with money/quantity overloads, quantifier parse/scope/CI-inheritance/queue-by projection, the member-access accessor tables, and the temporal/business operator matrices all match canon, including proof-obligation generation at rule/action positions (PRE0063/0100/0083/0113/0114, mincount and when-guard discharges). Serious findings: (1) exponent-notation literals are broken end-to-end — typed decimal instead of number, lane restriction unenforced, and the VALUE silently parses to 0 (discarded TryParse in ResolveNumericLiteral:191-215) which flows into proof folds (PRE0164 witnessed E=0) → soundness-relevant; (2) three declared-but-dead diagnostics = fail-open holes: FunctionArgConstraintViolation (round negative places / mid bounds never checked; [StaticallyPreventable]-linked → P11 hole), InvalidInterpolationCoercion (collections interpolate silently), KeyPresenceSafety (F for K generates no obligation in any position); (3) optional event-arg accesses generate no presence obligation at all; (4) ~startsWith/~endsWith first-arg ~string enforcement missing; (5) conditional ~string unification missing (CI drops through if/then/else); (6) qualifier metadata dropped through unary minus and not derived for money/quantity→price (over-rejects); (7) cross-currency money division rejected by an un-carved-out PRE0070 check despite spec+catalog declaring →exchangerate (clear oversight — the adjacent PRE0071 dimension check has exactly the needed carve-out); (8) contains-chaining and unordered-choice comparison reject via different mechanisms/codes than spec states; (9) expression-internal short-circuit narrowing (is-not-set-or / count==0-or disjunctions, presence ternary) is uniformly unimplemented — only when-guard GuardInPath narrows — leaving the spec's own recommended 'non-empty if present' idiom uncompilable for fields; (10) a 7-instance extension of the BUG-029 raw-message family. Seven canon-internal inconsistencies reported. BUG-030/031/032 folded by citation per instruction. Coverage caveats: temporal/business matrices probed at the spec-§3.6 summary level (full per-type tables belong to the datatypes auditor); PRE0163/PRE0050 arg-reference arms left to the name-binding area.

- **[fully]** Binary arithmetic + - * / on integer/decimal/number with int→decimal/int→number widening — `spec §3.6:1388,1402; Operations.cs kinds 10-34`
  - evidence: Probe: `I + J * 2 - 1 / 1 > 0`, `D + 0.5`, `I + D`, `I + N` all clean; catalog has all 25 same-lane + widening kinds; div-by-zero literal obligations Proved
- **[fully]** Modulo % (all numeric lanes) incl. divisor-zero obligation — `spec §2.1:840, §3.6:1388; OperationKind 14/19/24/29/34`
  - evidence: Probe: `I % 2 == 1` clean; `10 % 3.0` clean (Literal-proved divisor); `I % Z` with Z=0-able → PRE0083 'Division is unsafe'
- **[fully]** decimal↔number lane bridge enforcement (no implicit mixing) — `spec §3.6:1402, §3.7:1605`
  - evidence: Probe: `D + N` and `D * N` → PRE0018 'Expected a decimal value here, but got number'; money×number-field → PRE0018
- **[diverges]** Exponent-notation numeric literals (1.5e2): number-only typing, type error in integer/decimal context, correct value — `spec §1.3:621-629, §3.8:1632 ('exponent literal in integer/decimal context → TypeMismatch')`
  - diverges: Spec: exponent form is number-only, error elsewhere, value = mantissa×10^exp. Impl: typed integer/decimal by dot-presence, value silently 0, no lane enforcement — and the wrong 0 flows into proof-engine interval/default folds (PRE0164 witness E=0), so proofs reason on wrong constants (false-reject observed; false-accept plausible). Git: code from typed-literal slices (b7868d60, 82a92056, refactor f148ca21); no commit records dropping exponent support; the discarded TryParse is the tell — AI-co-authored oversight → cleanup, not decision
  - gap: Add exponent-aware parse (NumberStyles.Float) + number-lane-only enforcement diagnostic + expected-type propagation for number contexts; add proof-engine regression test that an exponent literal's value is honored
  - evidence: Probes: `rule N >= 0.0e0` → PRE0018 'Expected a number value, got decimal' (exponent literal typed decimal); `field I as integer default 1e2` / `field E as decimal default 1.5e2` accepted with NO TypeMismatch; PRE0164 then witnesses 'fails when E=0' — the literal VALUE silently parses to 0. Root cause: TypeChecker.Expressions.cs:191-215 ResolveNumericLiteral uses decimal.TryParse(NumberStyles.AllowDecimalPoint) / long.TryParse and DISCARDS the failed parse (`_ =`), yielding 0; no exponent branch exists
- **[fully]** String concatenation + (string+string, ~string mixes → string) — `spec §3.6:1389-1392,1499`

## Rules + ensures in all scoping forms: global rules, guarded rules (when), state-scoped ensures (in/to/from), event ensures (on E [when] ensure), because clauses + interpolation, guard semantics and scoping resolution per form

*Docs read: docs/language/precept-language-spec.md §0.6 (proof contract + implementation-status table, lines 195-260), §0.7, §0.8 (mandatory because, line 278+), §2.2 (rule declaration line 932, in/to/from dispatch line 940, state/event ensure line 991), §2.5 (interpolation reassembly line 1160), §3.5 (scope rules line 1332, event arg access line 1370), §3.6 (string interpolation line 1551), §3.8 (stateless/stateful cross-validation line 1773), §3A.1-3A.4 (lines 1847-1975); src/Precept/Language/Constructs.cs (RuleDeclaration, StateEnsure, EventEnsure entries + slot definitions lines 17-40); src/Precept/Language/Constraints.cs (ConstraintKind catalog, ByToken); docs/Working/bugs.md (BUG-023 through BUG-032)*

**Area summary:** Audited rules + ensures across all scoping forms against spec §2.2/§3.5/§3.8/§3A and §0.6, with 17 precept_compile probes. Grammar/parse surface is healthy: all anchors (rule, in/to/from ensure, on-event ensure), pre-verb guards, comma-list expansion, `any` wildcard, mandatory because, and the ConstraintKind catalog are fully implemented and catalog-driven. Five significant gaps found by probe: (1) rule and ensure CONDITIONS are never checked to be boolean — `rule Amount because ...` with an integer condition compiles clean (guards ARE checked); (2) the spec's field-scope restriction is unenforced — rules and state ensures can reference event args (`rule Submit.approver != \"\"` clean), since the PRE0050 gate only exists when a different event is in scope; (3) because-clause interpolation is a stub — the parser discards hole expressions at parse time (holes become literal '{}'), so undefined names, collections, and type errors in because-messages are silently accepted and the message text is unrecoverable for runtime rendering; (4) PRE0155 contradictory-rule detection ignores guards — provably disjoint guarded rules are flagged as contradictory (probe-confirmed false positive); (5) rule-sourced relational discharge diverges from the just-promoted spec text — `rule X > Y` discharges `Z / (X - Y)` only when the consuming row has NO guard of its own (either/or source selection at Strategies.cs:1127-1129; impl and spec from the same 2026-06-04 slice disagree — cleanup-grade). Also confirmed: PRE0128 mixed-wildcard diagnostic is dead code (parser cascade preempts it, same-commit mismatch from 2026-05-12); satisfiability/guard diagnostics (PRE0154/0155/0159/0082/0153) all fire but render raw TypedExpression AST dumps (BUG-029); defaults folding works both halves (PRE0164/PRE0115) with the known BUG-028 completeness gaps and PRE0115 missing a source span; established BUG-031 (guard-slot obligation hole) and BUG-032 (member-call-arg hole) folded in as rows. The spec §0.6 implementation-status table is stale in the impl-ahead direction (4 'specification-only' rows now live). to/from/in ensures get no compile-time proof against transitions (statically-guaranteed-failing transitions compile silently) — recorded factually for the fault-floor run to adjudicate.

Severity is Warning so not a hard reject, but it violates Proof philosophy #2 (proven violations only). Recent satisfiability-scan code (Slice 2c-i era, 2026-06-04, AI-co-authored) — guard-awareness was simply never built into the pair scan: cleanup/extension, not a deliberate decision

## States, transitions, events (+args/initial), event handlers, state entry/exit actions, action verbs (set/add/remove/enqueue/dequeue/push/pop/append/insert/put/clear + by/into/at variants), access modes (modify/omit/guarded/all/editable baseline)

*Docs read: docs/language/precept-language-spec.md §1.1 (Keywords: Actions :360, Outcomes :377, Access Modes :384, State Modifiers :417); docs/language/precept-language-spec.md §2.2 Declaration Grammar (:887-1087); docs/language/precept-language-spec.md §3.8 (Action statement validation :1698, Access mode validation :1719, Transition outcome validation :1766, Stateless/stateful cross-validation :1773); docs/language/precept-language-spec.md §3A.2 Outcomes (:1908), §3A.5 Entity Construction (:1973-2113), §4/§5 (:2125); docs/compiler/graph-analyzer.md (§2, §6.1 wildcard expansion, §6.2-6.5, §12); src/Precept/Language/Constructs.cs; src/Precept/Language/Actions.cs; src/Precept/Language/Outcomes.cs*

**Area summary:** Audited states/transitions/events/handlers/hooks/actions/access-modes against spec §§1.1, 2.2, 3.8, 3A.2, 3A.5, the graph-analyzer stage doc, and the Constructs/Actions/Outcomes catalogs, with 25+ precept_compile probes. The skeleton is strong: all declaration grammar, state modifiers with their structural graph checks (PRE0031/32/109/110/111/80/108/119), transition rows with guards/actions/outcomes, comma-list expansion, wildcards, construction-row machinery (PRE0093/94/142/144/145/146/147/148, AlwaysRejecting promotion), ensures in all four anchors with pre-verb guards, state hooks with proof-engine integration, action applicability (catalog-driven PRE0047/48), element-type checks for the simple collection verbs, emptiness/index/key-presence obligations, and the omit family (PRE0130/131/132/143, PRE0013, PRE0042) are FULLY implemented and probe-verified. The serious gaps cluster in three places. (1) Action operand typing is fail-open for every multi-operand v3 verb: `into` captures are never type-checked (string popped into integer compiles), `by` ordering keys unchecked, `put` key AND value unchecked, insert/removeAt index type unchecked, and dequeue-by's `by` operand is dropped from the typed model entirely — with canon itself split on whether dequeue-by is a selector or a capture. (2) Access-mode redundancy enforcement implements only one of the spec's three cases (rule 4b guarded-readonly and the readonly half of 4c are missing), and state-level `modify <computed> editable` misses ComputedFieldNotWritable. (3) Initial-event enforcement is keyed on construction ROWS rather than the `initial` declaration: a declared-initial event without an on-row escapes PRE0145/146/147 entirely, and an AI-co-authored (Copilot, f8b1febd) 'legacy initial path' silently accepts `from <Initial> on <InitialEvent>` transition rows in direct contradiction of the spec's fire-once guarantee — cleanup, not a decision. Cross-cutting: guard-expression interiors in ALL positions (transition guards, hook guards, access-mode guards) collect zero proof obligations (BUG-031 confirmed live — `when 300 / X > 5` with X provably 0 compiles clean), and because/reject message interpolation holes are never name-bound. One hard divergence needs an owner call: reject reasons are literal-only (catalog-codified) vs the spec's StringExpr. Nine canon-internal inconsistencies recorded separately.

## Name binder + type checker (resolution, shadowing, inference, qualifier compatibility, D26 taint, arity/member checking, computed-field ordering + cycles)

*Docs read: docs/compiler/name-binder.md (full); docs/compiler/type-checker.md (full, 1211 lines); docs/language/precept-language-spec.md §3–§3.10 (lines 1215–1845); docs/language/collection-types.md (default[...]/list-literal sections); docs/Working/bugs.md (full heading scan + BUG-020/021/022 detail); src/Precept/Pipeline/NameBinder.cs (full); src/Precept/Pipeline/TypeChecker.cs (entry + Pass1b/1c); src/Precept/Pipeline/TypeChecker.Expressions.cs (identifier resolution + qualifier compatibility)*

**Area summary:** Name binder + type checker are substantially implemented and architecturally faithful to canon (two-pass binding, catalog-driven resolution, D26 taint verified clean, scoping/shadowing/PRE0163 model exactly per spec, cycle detection with correct downstream-of-cycle discrimination). 35 probe programs via precept_compile ground every load-bearing row. Key gaps for the readiness plan: (1) SOUNDNESS — cross-counting-unit ops (each+box, each>box) compile clean and prove Proved: PRE0137 is dead code for declared operands, disabled by a test-green cleanup commit a03fcf4e while the stage doc still claims enforcement; plus BUG-020 enforcement half (field-reference bounds inert) and BUG-021 (min-direction set containment) remain live. (2) MISSING checks — rule and ensure conditions are never checked for boolean (non-boolean rules compile clean); computed-expression type vs field type unchecked (string <- integer compiles); FunctionArgConstraintViolation never emitted (round(x,-1) clean); exponent literal accepted in decimal context; collections accepted in string-interpolation holes; ChoiceMissingElementType/ChoiceElementTypeMismatch never fire on fields; two access-mode arms (computed-in-editable, redundant readonly) missing. (3) DIVERGES — list-literal defaults rejected on set/queue/stack/log/bag (canon documents them, even `default []` fails); binder still resolves bare args before fields internally (LS-facing SymbolTable disagrees with checker on collisions). (4) Latent fail-open — six unenrolled no-default-arm switches in this area (BUG-022 family), including the binder's ParsedExpression/ParsedAction walkers where a new DU subtype would silently lose all its references. Eight canon-internal inconsistencies reported separately, mostly stage-doc drift behind locked spec decisions.

## Graph analyzer (reachability, dead-end detection, dominators/required, terminal analysis, guard treatment, event coverage, outcome classification, field-write-site analysis, proof forwarding)

*Docs read: docs/compiler/graph-analyzer.md (full); docs/language/precept-language-spec.md §0.4–0.7 (esp. §0.5 Graph Analyzer Design Contract, lines 176–193) + state-modifier tables (lines 417–426, 919); docs/language/graph-analyzer-roadmap.md; docs/Working/bugs.md (graph-relevant scan — no open graph-analyzer bugs); src/Precept/Pipeline/GraphAnalyzer.cs + GraphAnalyzer.FieldWriteSites.cs + StateGraph.cs (catalog/source as machine-readable spec); src/Precept/Language/Modifiers.cs (StateModifierMeta/EventModifierMeta entries); src/Precept/Pipeline/ProofEngine.cs §S12 (graph-fact consumption contract)*

**Area summary:** Graph analyzer is the healthiest stage audited: all six shipped §0.5 contract capabilities are live and probe-confirmed (11 diagnostic codes verified end-to-end via precept_compile), catalog-driven modifier dispatch is genuine, guard overapproximation is canon-compliant-by-design, and FieldNeverSet (§6.7) matches its doc exactly including reachability filtering. Three substantive gaps: (1) IRREVERSIBLE IS UNDER-ENFORCED — spec §0.5 item 5 requires no-PATH-back reverse-reachability, impl checks only direct back-edges; a multi-hop return path (A→B[irreversible]→C→A) compiles clean (probe-confirmed; git shows AI-era impl under-delivery against the older owner-authored spec target → strengthen impl); (2) stateless precepts never emit UnhandledEvent for dead event declarations (probe-confirmed clean compile); (3) the proof-forwarding consumption contract diverges — only 2 of 5 fact subtypes are consumed, and the UNDOCUMENTED dead-end→dead-end obligation suppression marks runtime-executable transitions' obligations as vacuously Proved (flagged as a false-Proved candidate for the fault-floor track, recorded factually here). One stub: the EventModifierMeta.RequiredAnalysis dispatch arm is an empty placeholder. The 10 roadmap modifiers are missing-by-explicit-canon-deferral (Compiler 1.1), not drift. Remaining findings are a doc-drift cluster in graph-analyzer.md (stale OQ2/EventHandlers claim, stale GraphEvent.IsInitial note, Appendix missing 4 shipped codes, StateGraph shape missing EdgeProofStatuses/UnreachableRowFact) plus 4 spec-internal inconsistencies (dead-end definition, Lengauer-Tarjan naming, item 5 path-vs-edge, item 6 reject/no-transition wording) — reported, not resolved.

- **[fully]** BFS reachability from initial + UnreachableState diagnostic (PRE0080) — `spec §0.5 item 1 (line 184); graph-analyzer.md §6.2`
  - evidence: GraphAnalyzer.cs:409–458 ComputeReachability (BFS, paths, parents); probe: unreachable state D → PRE0080 Warning 'State D is unreachable from initial state A'. Tests: Analyze_UnreachableState_EmitsDiagnosticAndReachabilityFacts, Analyze_CycleInGraph_AllCycleStatesReachable, Analyze_DiamondGraph_MultiplePathsToSameState (GraphAnalyzerTests.cs, 35 tests).
- **[fully]** Guard overapproximation — all edges traversable regardless of `when` guards — `spec §0.5 Overapproximation rule (line 191); graph-analyzer.md §2 Out of Scope (guard analysis), §13`
  - evidence: GraphEdge carries only HasGuard (StateGraph.cs:52–58); BFS ignores guards. Probe: B reachable only via `when X > 5` edge → no unreachable diagnostic for B or C. CANON-COMPLIANT by design.
- **[fully]** Terminal state identification + terminal constraint check (PRE0109 TerminalStateHasOutgoingEdges) — `spec §0.5 item 2 (line 185), state-modifier table line 421; graph-analyzer.md §6.4`
  - evidence: GetStateFlags derives IsTerminal from StateModifierMeta.AllowsOutgoing (GraphAnalyzer.cs:670–697, catalog-driven); BuildTerminalViolations excludes self-edges (GraphAnalyzer.cs:606–624) so reject/no-transition rows on terminals are legal. Probe: terminal B with B→A edge → PRE0109 Error; wildcard `no transition` row → clean.
- **[fully]** Structural sink detection — reachable non-terminal state with zero outgoing rows (PRE0119 StructuralSinkState) — `spec §0.5 items 2–3; graph-analyzer.md — NOT DOCUMENTED (absent from §6.2 and Appendix)`
  - evidence: GraphAnalyzer.cs:112–127 ('Message A'); probe: no-terminal precept, sink B → PRE0119 Warning 'State B has no outgoing transitions and is not marked terminal'. Test: Analyze_NoTerminalStates_StructuralSinkFires_DeadEndDoesNot.
- **[fully]** Dead-end state detection — reverse-reachability from terminals (PRE0108 DeadEndState) — `spec §0.5 item 3 (line 186); graph-analyzer.md §6.2 dead-end algorithm + OQ1 resolution`
  - evidence: ComputeDeadEnds reverse-BFS from terminals (GraphAnalyzer.cs:460–495); gated on ≥1 terminal declared; structural sinks excluded from Message B (GraphAnalyzer.cs:129–149). Probe: B with self-loop only, terminal C reachable from A → PRE0108 Warning. Tests: Analyze_DeadEndState_EmitsWarningAndDeadEndFact, Analyze_MultipleDeadEndStates_AllReported, Analyze_DeadEndState_WithOutgoingTransitions_FiresMessageB, Analyze_SingleStateInitialAndTerminal_ReachableNotDeadEnd.
- **[fully]** Dominator computation + required-modifier verification (PRE0111 RequiredStateDoesNotDominateTerminal) — `spec §0.5 item 4 (line 187), modifier table line 422; graph-analyzer.md §6.3, §10.3`
