Probe: required B bypassable via A→Skip→C → PRE0111 Warning + RelatedSpans naming undominated terminals (GraphAnalyzer.cs:267–285). Test: Analyze_RequiredState_ProducesDominanceFact.
- **[partially]** Irreversible-modifier enforcement (PRE0110 IrreversibleStateHasBackEdge) — `spec §0.5 item 5 (line 188): 'there is no PATH from this state back to any ancestor state'; modifier table line 423: 'no path back to any ancestor state'; graph-analyzer.md §6.4 + resolved-note (BFS-ancestor)`
  - diverges: Spec (owner-authored 2026-04-29, commit a52282a8, pre-implementation) demands no-path-back — i.e. genuine reverse-reachability ('Reverse-reachability. Required for irreversible'). Impl (2026-05-07, 67d42b2a) shipped a direct-back-edge-only check; the same-day team-autonomous doc resolution (Wave 4) canonized 'BFS-tree ancestor' for the ancestor notion but never addressed direct-edge-vs-any-path. Git read: AI-era implementation under-delivery against the pre-existing owner spec target, not a deliberate weakening decision — cleanup direction is to strengthen the impl, not relax the spec.
  - gap: Implement true reverse-reachability: forward-reach set from each irreversible state intersected with its ancestor set (or with all states ordered-before it) must be empty; flag the first edge on any returning path. Reverse adjacency and BFS machinery already exist (ComputeDeadEnds). Also decide BFS-tree-ancestor vs any-earlier-reachable-state as the ancestor notion and align spec+doc wording.
  - evidence: BuildBackEdgeViolations inspects only DIRECT edges whose FromState is the irreversible state, against BFS-tree ancestors (GraphAnalyzer.cs:626–668). Probe 1 (direct): A→B(irreversible)→A → PRE0110 Error. Probe 2 (multi-hop): A→B(irreversible)→C→A, A→Go→B re-enterable → compiles CLEAN, no PRE0110 — the irreversibility claim is structurally violated but unflagged.

## Proof engine + diagnostic system (spec §0.6 items 1–13, obligation kinds, strategies, diagnostic catalog/PRE codes, Roslyn analyzers)

*Docs read: docs/language/precept-language-spec.md §0.6–0.8 (lines 195–310); docs/compiler/proof-engine.md (full); docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md (full); docs/Working/bugs.md (full)*

**Area summary:** Proof engine: of the 13 spec §0.6 obligations, items 1/4/5 are fully implemented; 7/8/9/10 are implemented but the spec status table still calls them specification-only (4 stale rows; probes confirm PRE0155/0154/0082/0153 emit — all with raw-AST message dumps, BUG-029); item 12 is genuinely missing (UnreachableRowFact produced, zero consumers; probe shows no sharpened reachability); items 2/3/6/11/13 are partial (relational-contradiction warnings, two-tier divisor distinction, BUG-021 min-only set gap, BUG-028 fold coarseness, no per-proof attribution carrier). All 11 strategies + IndexBounds path are live; KeyPresence (PRE0099/0101) is a dead path (no obligation generator, no lookup keyed accessor). Confirmed live soundness holes at HEAD: BUG-031 (guard conditions never walked — `when X / Y > 0` compiles clean with zero obligations, probe-confirmed), BUG-032 (member-access arguments not recursed), mincount-at-birth seed, BUG-030 cross-unit cancellation (documented deferred policy), plus inherited qualifier-mutation/presence-wrapping/choice-default findings. Diagnostic system: 164-code registry, exhaustive switch, Create factory, 15-fault [StaticallyPreventable] chain, and 29 Roslyn analyzers (PRECEPT0001–0031) are all real and wired; ~16 codes are never-emitted stubs (temporal/parser-precision/choice/key-presence clusters per Gate1AllowList, incl. 2 retired codes one of which is still a fault target); Gate2 test-coverage gate is currently a no-op blanket allow-list. Diagnostic-system.md and proof-engine.md carry heavy internal drift (stale enum/fault listings, 3 conflicting strategy counts, 3 conflicting code counts, wrong scanner-Pattern-3 claim). Message quality: PRE0164/0136/0114 are domain-grade; PRE0082/0153/0154/0155/0159 dump ASTs; PRE0135 leaks a '?' placeholder.

## Gap-fill: precept header & top-level dispatch, parser error recovery (§2.6), §2.7 parser diagnostics, Compilation orchestration + Precept Builder (pipeline tail), grammar generation, catalog meta-surfaces (catalog-system.md drift, SlotValue/ParsedConstruct contract, PreceptHeader construct), spec §0.2–0.4/§3A.6/Tooling-Snapshot dispositions, §1.7 numbering

*Docs read: docs/language/precept-language-spec.md (§0.1–0.4 lines 76–175, §1.6–1.8 lines 700–790, §2.2 lines 880–930, §2.5–2.7 lines 1150–1213, §3A.6 line 2115, header inventory); docs/compiler-and-runtime-design.md §9 Compilation (598), §10 Precept Builder (664); docs/compiler/parser.md (§Output Shape 78–133, §Failure Modes and Recovery 389–419); docs/compiler/diagnostic-system.md (registry lines 200–235, code-66 retirement note 410); docs/compiler/grammar-generator.md (status block); docs/language/catalog-system.md (Level 1 map ~258, Level 3 ~702, Catalog Inventory ~991, Exhaustiveness Strategies ~925); docs/language/precept-grammar.md (status block)*

**Area summary:** All 11 uncovered spec sections and 7 uncovered catalog surfaces now have owning rows, evidence-grounded by 12 precept_compile probes plus source/git reads. Headline findings: (1) the pipeline tail is the largest real gap — Precept Builder is a stub (From() gates on HasErrors then wraps SemanticIndex raw; no descriptor tables, plans, or indexes; most surface throws NotImplementedException) and the §9 contract-digest/ContractDiff is missing, while Compilation orchestration itself fully matches canon (all stages always run, deterministic aggregation order). (2) Four §2.7 parser diagnostics are dead code silently lost in the 004eede1 catalog-first parser rewrite — UnexpectedKeyword and InvalidCallTarget degrade to generic PRE0009, and the two choice codes guard validation that no longer exists at all: `choice(\"a\",\"b\")` without `of T` and `choice of string(1,2)` both compile clean (new validation hole, broader than the known choice-default-membership gap). (3) Code 14 (EventHandlerDoesNotSupportGuard) was deliberately retired (archived constructor-semantics decision; EventRow now carries a guard slot and guarded handlers compile clean) but both spec §2.7 and diagnostic-system.md still list it live — doc drift the other way. (4) Parser recovery: sync-point resync is fully implemented and catalog-derived (LeadingTokens = exactly the spec's 9 tokens); missing-node insertion diverges in representation (no IsMissing, found-token span instead of zero-length) — spec text predates the parser rewrite. (5) Header/dispatch: top-level dispatch is fully catalog-derived (ByLeadingToken, no parallel keyword lists — the CLAUDE.md non-negotiable holds); the precept header parses but a missing header is silently tolerated ('Unknown'), the name is never bound into the semantic model, and a second `precept` header is silently swallowed. (6) Field multi-name shorthand, `<-` computed arrow, and interpolation reassembly are fully implemented. (7) Meta-doc rot: parser.md's locked 17-subtype SlotValue decision vs 20 in code; catalog-system.md Level 1 counts (3 stale) and a CS8509 dispatch-site table citing nonexistent methods. Explicit dispositions recorded: §3A.6 inspection (runtime stub, out of scope), Tooling Surface Snapshot (tooling-area audit), Quickstart/SyntaxReference/SemanticTokenTypes (tooling content), runtime fault raising (evaluator); tmLanguage generation ruled IN scope and verified implemented (gap: no regenerate-and-diff freshness test). Eight canon-internal inconsistencies reported, including the §1.7 numbering gap.

Probe: header-less source (`field A as string default "x"`) compiles clean — success:true, summary precept name 'Unknown', NO missing-header diagnostic. The precept name is never bound into the semantic model: only tools/Precept.Mcp/Tools/CompileTool.cs:77 reads the header construct (FirstOrDefault); NameBinder/SemanticIndex/Compilation carry no precept name.
